2.1.5. San Juan Capistrano Groundwater Treatment Plant
The San Juan Capistrano Groundwater Treatment Plant is located at 32470
Paseo Adelanto, San Juan Capistrano, California and treats up to 5.2 MGD of
extracted groundwater to produce potable water. Groundwater treatment
includes preliminary filtration (sand separators); iron and manganese
pretreatment (potassium permanganate and sodium hypochlorite addition
followed by green-sand filtration); chemical addition (addition of sulfuric acid,
sodium bisulfite and scale inhibitor); cartridge filtration; RO treatment; aeration;
pH adjustment (sodium hydroxide and ammonia hydroxide); and disinfection
(sodium hypochlorite). The San Juan Capistrano Groundwater Treatment Plant
discharges up to 2.2 MGD of the waste brine from the RO to the Chiquita Land
Outfall, which then discharges to the SJCOO. From February 2019 to January
2020, the reported average effluent daily flow from the San Juan Capistrano
Groundwater Treatment Plant to the SJCOO was 0.52 MGD.
2.1.6. South Coast Water District Groundwater Recovery Facility
The SCWD Groundwater Recovery Facility is located at 33750 Stonehill Drive,
Dana Point, California. At this facility, SCWD treats extracted groundwater from
the Lower San Juan Groundwater Basin to produce potable supply. Treatment
includes pretreatment (threshold inhibitor and cartridge filtration); RO treatment;
decarbonation (pH adjustment); and stabilization/disinfection (sodium
hypochlorite, aqueous ammonia, and sodium hydroxide). A portion of the
extracted groundwater bypasses RO treatment and is treated for iron and
manganese removal. The SCWD discharges up to 0.6 MGD of the resulting
waste brine from the RO and iron/manganese backwash to the Chiquita Land
Outfall, which discharges to the SJCOO. From February 2019 to January 2020,
the reported average effluent daily flow from the SCWD Groundwater Recovery
Facility to the SJCOO was 0.16 MGD.
2.1.7. Segunda Deshecha Runoff Plant
The Segunda Deshecha Runoff Plant is located at 3115 Avenida Pico, San
Clemente, California. At this facility, the City of San Clemente diverts up to 1.01
MGD of dry weather urban runoff flows from the Segunda Deshecha (M02) Flood
Control Channel through the Segunda Deshecha Runoff Plant for treatment by
preliminary screening and pressure filtration. The Segunda Deshecha Runoff
Plant discharges up to 1.0 MGD of treated urban runoff to the San Clemente
Land Outfall for conveyance to the SJCOO. The filter backwash water from the
treatment facility is discharged into the San Clemente WRP sewer collection
system. From February 2019 to October 2021, the City of San Clemente reported
no discharge from the Segunda Deshecha Runoff Plant to the SJCOO.

----- SYSTEM PROMPT (paste into the system/custom-instructions field) -----
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
You are an expert in wastewater treatments.

Inputs:
A wastewater treatment plant permit extract
Ontology lists: equipments, processes and roles (with mandatory relationships)

Task:
Using only the wastewater treatment plant's permit extract, find all the future, implemented or past treatments mentioned to be used in the Civita Water Reclamation Facility facility. A treatment must be described by at least an equipment or a process from the given ontology lists.
You may ignore irrelevant context.
Think step by step:
1. Extract explicitly from the permit text the treatments mentioned for the specific Civita Water Reclamation Facility facility (if it is used in another facility or off-site it should be mentionned in the "location" field of the output JSON)
2. Match each treatment find with an equipment and/or one or many processes from the ontology lists
3. Complete with specification of the treatment with roles and substances if explicitly mentioned in the text
4. Determine if the treatment is currently implemented and used in the facility ("present"), planned for the future ("future") or has been shut or removed ("past")
5. Structure the output as a JSON file as requested

Output:
Your entire response must be a single JSON object: an "items" array with one object per treatment unit, using this structure:
{
    "items": [
        {
            "Equipment": string|null,
            "Process": string[]|null,
            "Role": string[]|null,
            "Substance": string[]|null,
            "Implementation": "present"|"future"|"past",
            "Location": "on-site"|"off-site"|null,
            "Sentence": string
        }
    ]
}
Emit one object per distinct treatment step. A municipal plant typically yields ~8-20 steps. Each object must include exactly one Equipment (or one Process if no equipment is named), plus only the processes that occur in that unit. Processes in different units belong in different objects.

Field meaning and allowed cardinality:
- Equipment: equipment name from ontology. Exactly one string, or null only if no specific equipment is identifiable. Populate whenever a physical unit or vessel is mentioned or clearly implied.
- Process: process names from ontology. Null or an array of 1+ strings. Populate whenever a treatment process is described, even if Equipment is also set.
- Role: role names from ontology. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Substance: substance names explicitly mentioned in text. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Rule: at least one of Equipment or Process must be non-null. Both should be set when both are relevant.
- Implementation: a single string, one of "present", "future", "past".
- Location: a single string, one of "on-site" or "off-site"
- Sentence: shortest excerpt supporting the extraction from the permit — a phrase, not necessarily a full sentence.

All equipment, process, role and substance names must be exactly as listed in the ontology sections (EQUIPMENTS, PROCESSES, ROLES, SUBSTANCES). Use only the most specific applicable term; its parents are inferred, so do not also list them (e.g. "CentrifugalThickener", not "CentrifugalThickener" and "Thickener").

Do not include example processes listed in boilerplate language.

PROCESSES:
Process | A process applied to water treatment.
PhysicalProcess | A unit process that relies on physical forces to separate or treat water.
Separation | The most generic process for separation of any two constituents. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Elutriation | A process that uses a stream of fluid flowing upward to separate a mixture of particles based on their size, shape, and density. | SubProcessOf: Separation
Screening | A unit process that removes large solids from water, such as leaves, branches, and other debris. | SubProcessOf: Separation
Sedimentation | A unit process that allows solids to settle out of water by gravity. | SubProcessOf: Separation

RapidSandFiltration | A filtration process that uses a bed of sand to remove impurities at high flow rates. | SubProcessOf: MediaFiltration
Flotation | A unit process that uses buoyancy to separate solids from water, often using air bubbles. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
GasTransfer | A unit process that transfers gases, such as oxygen or carbon dioxide, into or out of water. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Aeration | A unit process that aerates (i.e., transfers oxygen into water). | SubProcessOf: GasTransfer
Mixing | A unit process that mixes water with chemicals or other substances to achieve a desired effect. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Recirculation | A process that returns a portion of a treated or intermediate flow back upstream to maintain hydraulic, solids, or biological performance. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Thickening | A process to increase the solids content of a sludge or slurry. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Dewatering | A process to remove or separate water from another material, typically solids. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Drying | Drying of biosolids to reduce moisture content. | SubProcessOf: Dewatering, Evaporation
Centrifugation | A process that uses centrifugal force to separate substances of different densities. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess

Example 1:
Permit text extract: "2.1.3. Wastewater Treatment. The treatment plant provides treatment consisting of influent screening and grit removal, primary clarification, advanced secondary treatment with an activated sludge process configured and operated for biological nutrient removal (BNR), secondary clarifiers, mono-media gravity filtration, and disinfection with chlorine (sodium hypochlorite), and dechlorination (sodium bisulfite).
    2.1.3.5. Disinfection. Sodium hypochlorite and ammonia are metered into the filter effluent at the head of four serpentine chlorine contact channels to produce a solution of chloramines for disinfection. As the effluent leaves the contact channels, its chlorine residual is measured, and an appropriate amount of sodium bisulfite is added to neutralize the hypochlorite.
    2.1.4. Sludge and Biosolids Management. Sludge from primary and secondary clarification operations is processed using Temperature Phased Anaerobic Digestion (TPAD) with thermophilic and mesophilic anaerobic digesters that are operated in series. The process consists of primary sludge screening, dissolved air flotation (DAF) thickening of primary and secondary sludges, and TPAD. Digested sludge then undergoes dewatering using open air drying beds or mechanical dewatering using centrifuges.
    Planned Changes: 2.5.2. Digested Sludge Dewatering Facility. This project will build a new digested sludge dewatering facility, including centrifuges and polymer storage and dosing equipment."

Find all the treatment processes explicitely used in the Civita Water Reclamation Facility facility. Here is the permit extract:
        [Page 26]
II. FACILITY DESCRIPTION
A. Description of Civita Water Reclamation Facility.
The Facility is located at the northwest corner of Friars Road and Russell Parkway in
the City of San Diego, California, Attachment A, and is being constructed as part of the
2 The City of San Diego, County of San Diego, and San Diego County Water Authority’s 2019
San Diego Integrated Regional Water Management Plan,
https://sdirwmp.org/pdf/SDIRWM_Final_2019_IRWM_Plan_compiled_2Jul19.pdf
3 See Practical Vision for “Strategy for Achieving a Sustainable Local Water Supply”
http://www.waterboards.ca.gov/sandiego/water_issues/Practical_Vision/index.shtml
C-2
[Page 27]
Attachment C August 12, 2020
Information Sheet
Civita Development. The Facility will be a scalping plant with no solids treated onsite,
and sludge processing occurring offsite at downstream City of San Diego facilities. The
projected average wastewater flow from the entire Civita Development at buildout is
1.684 mgd of raw sewage treated to secondary effluent standards. The Facility will be
designed to produce a daily maximum of 0.33 mgd and a daily average of 0.26 mgd of
disinfected tertiary recycled water. The disinfected tertiary recycled water will be
produced from secondary treated effluent which will be approximately 15% of the Civita
build out wastewater flow. The treatment processes will include influent diversion and
pumping, screening, an aeration tank, a membrane bioreactor process, and a chlorine
disinfection system. The headworks will be designed to divert and pump flow to the start
of the treatment process. A drum screen will be installed to provide primary treatment
and protection of downstream processes. The membrane bioreactor process combines
activated sludge (secondary treatment) with membrane filtration (tertiary treatment),
with the membrane units installed within the activated sludge reactor membrane tank.
Within the activated sludge reactor membrane tank clean water will be separated from
sludge and extracted through flat plate membranes. Disinfection will be accomplished
utilizing a chlorine disinfection system consisting of two dosing pumps installed on a
chemical feed skid which will provide each pump with a calibration column, pressure
relief valve, and a pulsing damper. A 480,000-gallon storage tank will serve as effluent
storage, a forebay for the effluent pumps, and provide for in-plant water needs.
B. Recycled Water Use.
Recycled water produced at the Facility will be used onsite for landscape irrigation on
the Civita Development Project.
The Facility wil

----- SYSTEM PROMPT (paste into the system/custom-instructions field) -----
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
You are an expert in wastewater treatments.

Inputs:
A wastewater treatment plant permit extract
Ontology lists: equipments, processes and roles (with mandatory relationships)

Task:
Using only the wastewater treatment plant's permit extract, find all the future, implemented or past treatments mentioned to be used in the Housing Authority Cnty of Merced - Los Banos facility. A treatment must be described by at least an equipment or a process from the given ontology lists.
You may ignore irrelevant context.
Think step by step:
1. Extract explicitly from the permit text the treatments mentioned for the specific Housing Authority Cnty of Merced - Los Banos facility (if it is used in another facility or off-site it should be mentionned in the "location" field of the output JSON)
2. Match each treatment find with an equipment and/or one or many processes from the ontology lists
3. Complete with specification of the treatment with roles and substances if explicitly mentioned in the text
4. Determine if the treatment is currently implemented and used in the facility ("present"), planned for the future ("future") or has been shut or removed ("past")
5. Structure the output as a JSON file as requested

Output:
Your entire response must be a single JSON object: an "items" array with one object per treatment unit, using this structure:
{
    "items": [
        {
            "Equipment": string|null,
            "Process": string[]|null,
            "Role": string[]|null,
            "Substance": string[]|null,
            "Implementation": "present"|"future"|"past",
            "Location": "on-site"|"off-site"|null,
            "Sentence": string
        }
    ]
}
Emit one object per distinct treatment step. A municipal plant typically yields ~8-20 steps. Each object must include exactly one Equipment (or one Process if no equipment is named), plus only the processes that occur in that unit. Processes in different units belong in different objects.

Field meaning and allowed cardinality:
- Equipment: equipment name from ontology. Exactly one string, or null only if no specific equipment is identifiable. Populate whenever a physical unit or vessel is mentioned or clearly implied.
- Process: process names from ontology. Null or an array of 1+ strings. Populate whenever a treatment process is described, even if Equipment is also set.
- Role: role names from ontology. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Substance: substance names explicitly mentioned in text. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Rule: at least one of Equipment or Process must be non-null. Both should be set when both are relevant.
- Implementation: a single string, one of "present", "future", "past".
- Location: a single string, one of "on-site" or "off-site"
- Sentence: shortest excerpt supporting the extraction from the permit — a phrase, not necessarily a full sentence.

All equipment, process, role and substance names must be exactly as listed in the ontology sections (EQUIPMENTS, PROCESSES, ROLES, SUBSTANCES). Use only the most specific applicable term; its parents are inferred, so do not also list them (e.g. "CentrifugalThickener", not "CentrifugalThickener" and "Thickener").

Do not include example processes listed in boilerplate language.

Ontology lists:

PROCESSES:
Process | A process applied to water treatment.
PhysicalProcess | A unit process that relies on physical forces to separate or treat water.
Separation | The most generic process for separation of any two constituents. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Elutriation | A process that uses a stream of fluid flowing upward to separate a mixture of particles based on their size, shape, and density. | SubProcessOf: Separation
Screening | A unit process that removes large solids from water, such as leaves, branches, and other debris. | SubProcessOf: Separation
Sedimentation | A unit process that allows solids to settle out of water by gravity. | SubProcessOf: Separation
Stripping | Gas stripping for removal of dissolved gases such as ammonia and volatile organic compounds. | SubProcessOf: Separation
Filtration | A unit process that removes smaller particles from water by passing it through a filter. | SubProcessOf: Separation
MediaFiltration | A filtration process that uses a bed of media (as opposed to a membrane-based process). | SubProcessOf: Filtration
SlowSandFiltration | A filtration process that uses a bed of sand to remove impurities at low flow rates. | SubProcessOf: MediaFiltration
RapidSandFiltration | A filtration process that uses a bed of sand to remove impurities at high flow rates. | SubProcessOf: MediaFiltration
Flotation | A unit process that uses buoyancy to separate solids from water, often using air bubbles. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
GasTransfer | A unit process that transfers gases, such as oxygen or carbon dioxide, into or out of water. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Aeration | A unit process that aerates (i.e., transfers oxygen into water). | SubProcessOf: GasTransfer
Mixing | A unit process that mixes water with chemicals or other substances to achieve a desired effect. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Recirculation | A process that returns a portion of a treated or intermediate flow back upstream to maintain hydraulic, solids, or biological performance. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Thickening | A process to increase the solids content of a sludge or slurry. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Dewatering | A process to remove or separate water from another material, typically solids. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Drying | Drying of biosolids to reduce moisture content. | SubProcessOf: Dewatering, Evaporation
Centrifugation | A process that uses centrifugal force to separate substances of different densities. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Solidification | A process of turning a liquid into a solid (e.g., freezing). | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Crystallization | A process of forming solid crystals from a solution. | SubProcessOf: Solidification
Evaporation | A process of turning a liquid into a vapor. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Condensation | A process of turning a vapor into a liquid. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Adsorption | A process by which substances bind to a surface. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
GranularActivatedCarbon | A process that uses an activated carbon filter to adsorb impurities from water. | SubProcessOf: Adsorption, Filtration
MembraneProcess | A process that uses a semi-permeable membrane to separate substances. | SubProcessOf: Filtration
ReverseOsmosis | A core membrane separation process for desalination and advanced purification. | SubProcessOf: MembraneProcess
ClosedCircuitReverseOsmosis | A specific RO configuration explored for optimization, capable of adapting to unmeasured changes like feed salinity. | SubProcessOf: ReverseOsmosis
OsmoticallyAssistedReverseOsmosis | A process with validated Computational Fluid Dynamics (CFD) models for cost optimization. | SubProcessOf: ReverseOsmosis
FeedReversalReverseOsmosis | A dynamic and cyclic design to mitigate scaling and balance salt/foulant load, enabling high recovery. | SubProcessOf: ReverseOsmosis
MembraneDistillation | A thermal membrane process, often subject to CFD modeling for optimization of heat and mass transfer. | SubProcessOf: MembraneProcess
Electrodialysis | A process that uses ion-exchange membranes and an electric potential to separate ions from water. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess, PhysicalProcess
ElectroDialyticCrystallization | A novel integrated process combining electrodialysis and crystallization for energy-efficient Zero-Liquid Discharge (ZLD) by maintaining a saturated brine stream. | SubProcessOf: Electrodialysis, Crystallization
Comminution | Process of reducing solid materials into smaller particles through mechanical forces such as crushing, grinding, impact, shear, or compression. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
BiosolidsDisposal | Generic process for unspecified disposal of biosolids (e.g., incineration, land application, landfilling, etc.). | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Composting | Biological degradation of organic materials under controlled aerobic conditions. | SubProcessOf: BiosolidsDisposal
LandApplication | Beneficial reuse of biosolids by spreading on agricultural land. | SubProcessOf: BiosolidsDisposal
Landfill | Disposal of biosolids in a landfill. | SubProcessOf: BiosolidsDisposal
ChemicalProcess | A unit process that involves the addition of chemicals to water to achieve a desired effect.
ChemicalAddition | Addition of chemicals, such as coagulant, polymer, disinfectant, etc. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
Coagulation | A unit process that adds chemicals to water to cause small particles to clump together. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalAddition
Electrocoagulation | An electrified pretreatment technology using sacrificial anodes to release coagulant precursors for contaminant removal. | SubProcessOf: Coagulation, PhysicalProcess
Flocculation | A unit process that gently mixes water to allow coagulated particles to form larger clumps. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess, Mixing
UVDisinfection | A disinfection process that uses ultraviolet light to inactivate microorganisms. | SubProcessOf: Disinfection
Disinfection | A unit process that uses chemicals or other methods to kill or inactivate harmful microorganisms in water. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
Chlorination | A disinfection process that uses chlorine or chlorine compounds. | SubProcessOf: Disinfection
Dechlorination | A post-disinfection process that removes excess chlorine compounds. | SubProcessOf: Disinfection
ThermalDisinfection | Heat-based pathogen inactivation such as pasteurization. | SubProcessOf: Disinfection
pHAdjustment | A unit process that adjusts the pH of water to a desired level using chemicals. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalAddition
Neutralization | pH adjustment to neutral, typically with a base, used in processes like high-density sludge treatment. | SubProcessOf: pHAdjustment
Oxidation | A unit process that adds oxidizing agents to water to remove contaminants or improve water quality. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
AdvancedOxidation | Processes combining UV light with oxidants (e.g., UV-AOP) to destroy contaminants. | SubProcessOf: Oxidation
Electrooxidation | An advanced oxidation process often combined with EC to enhance contaminant removal and inactivation, particularly for viruses. | SubProcessOf: Oxidation, PhysicalProcess
Ozonation | A water treatment process that uses ozone as an oxidant. | SubProcessOf: Oxidation
Softening | Chemical precipitation of calcium/magnesium | SubProcessOf: ChemicalAddition
ChemicalPrecipitation | Removal of nutrients or metals using coagulants | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
Reduction | Mechanism by which contaminants are transformed through the gain of electrons. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
SolventExtraction | A non-membrane, non-thermal method using a solvent to selectively extract water from highly saline brines. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
HighDensitySludge | A method for treating acid mine drainage involving neutralization. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess, PhysicalProcess
IonExchange | A process in which ions are exchanged between a solution and an ion-exchange resin or membrane. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess, PhysicalProcess
Electrolysis | A process that uses a direct electric current to drive an otherwise non-spontaneous chemical reaction. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
Combustion | A process that combusts biosolids or biogas. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess, PhysicalProcess
FluidizedBedIncineration | A process that combusts a constantly moving bed of biosolids. | SubProcessOf: Combustion
MultipleHearthIncineration | A process that combusts biosolids as they move through a series of stacked hearths with controlled air flow. | SubProcessOf: Combustion
Cogeneration | A process that combusts biosolids or biogas to produce electricity and heat simultaneously. | SubProcessOf: Combustion
BiologicalProcess | A unit process that utilizes biological activity for water treatment.
Nitrification | Process by which ammonia is oxidized to nitrite and subsequently nitrate. | SubProcessOf: BiologicalProcess
Denitrification | Process by which nitrate is deoxidized to nitrogen gas and nitrogen oxides. | SubProcessOf: BiologicalProcess
FiveStageBardenpho | A five-stage biological nutrient removal process specifically designed for nitrogen and phosphorus. | SubProcessOf: ActivatedSludge, Nitrification, Denitrification
FourStageBardenpho | A four-stage biological nutrient removal process with two anoxic and two aerobic zones. | SubProcessOf: ActivatedSludge, Nitrification, Denitrification
ActivatedSludge | Aerobic suspended-growth biological treatment | SubProcessOf: Separation, BiologicalProcess
AO | Two-stage process with anoxic zone followed by aerobic zone for biological nitrogen removal. | SubProcessOf: ActivatedSludge, Nitrification, Denitrification
MLE | Modification of AO a with internal recycle. | SubProcessOf: AO
A2O | Anaerobic-anoxic-aerobic process for simultaneous biological nitrogen and phosphorus removal. | SubProcessOf: ActivatedSludge, Nitrification, Denitrification
UCT | Variant of A2O with modified recycle streams for phosphorus removal. | SubProcessOf: A2O
Biofiltration | A filtration method that uses biological processes to remove contaminants. | SubProcessOf: BiologicalProcess, Filtration
TricklingFiltration | Trickling filter using any media (e.g., plastic, sand, gravel, etc.) | SubProcessOf: Biofiltration
BiologicallyActiveFiltration | A biologically activated filtration column. | SubProcessOf: Biofiltration
Microfiltration | 0.1 to 1 micron-scale filtration | SubProcessOf: Filtration
Ultrafiltration | 0.01 to 0.1 micron-scale filtration | SubProcessOf: Filtration
Backwashing | A cleaning process in which water, and sometimes air, is driven backward through a filter or membrane system to remove accumulated solids. | SubProcessOf: Cleaning
AirScouring | A cleaning process that uses injected air to dislodge accumulated foulants or solids from filter media or membrane surfaces. | SubProcessOf: Cleaning
Purging | A process that flushes retained liquid, solids, or gas from equipment or piping to restore operating conditions or prepare for another cycle. | SubProcessOf: Cleaning
Digestion | A biological process that breaks down organic matter over a long duration in a tank. | SubProcessOf: BiologicalProcess
Hydrolysis | A process reaction in which a water molecule is consumed to split a larger molecule into smaller fragments. | SubProcessOf: ChemicalProcess
ThermalHydrolysis | A process that breaks down complex organic matter into soluble compounds using heat and pressure. | SubProcessOf: Hydrolysis
Cleaning | A maintenance-oriented process that removes accumulated solids, foulants, or residual materials from water treatment equipment. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
AnaerobicDigestion | A biological process that breaks down organic matter in the absence of oxygen. | SubProcessOf: Digestion
AerobicDigestion | A biological process that breaks down organic matter in the presence of oxygen. | SubProcessOf: Digestion

Examples of treatments mentioned in permit text and the corresponding JSON output:
########################################################################
Example 1:
Permit text extract: "2.1.3. Wastewater Treatment. The treatment plant provides treatment consisting of influent screening and grit removal, primary clarification, advanced secondary treatment with an activated sludge process configured and operated for biological nutrient removal (BNR), secondary clarifiers, mono-media gravity filtration, and disinfection with chlorine (sodium hypochlorite), and dechlorination (sodium bisulfite).
    2.1.3.5. Disinfection. Sodium hypochlorite and ammonia are metered into the filter effluent at the head of four serpentine chlorine contact channels to produce a solution of chloramines for disinfection. As the effluent leaves the contact channels, its chlorine residual is measured, and an appropriate amount of sodium bisulfite is added to neutralize the hypochlorite.
    2.1.4. Sludge and Biosolids Management. Sludge from primary and secondary clarification operations is processed using Temperature Phased Anaerobic Digestion (TPAD) with thermophilic and mesophilic anaerobic digesters that are operated in series. The process consists of primary sludge screening, dissolved air flotation (DAF) thickening of primary and secondary sludges, and TPAD. Digested sludge then undergoes dewatering using open air drying beds or mechanical dewatering using centrifuges.
    Planned Changes: 2.5.2. Digested Sludge Dewatering Facility. This project will build a new digested sludge dewatering facility, including centrifuges and polymer storage and dosing equipment."

        [Page 0]
DISCHARGE DESCRIPTION
The Facility is located at 18926 Henry Miller Road, Los Banos in Merced County, approximately
one mile north of the Los Banos City limits. The Facility treats domestic wastewater for 48
rental units which are typically occupied from mid-May through mid-November. While occupied,
daily flow into the treatment system is approximately 14,352 gpd. The Facility consists of a lift
station and three wastewater ponds, one of which is equipped with an aerator. It is reported
that wastewater never leaves the first pond.
[Page 1]
Rosa Vazquez - 2 - 10 May 2019
Los Banos Migrant Center

effluent biochemical oxygen demand (BOD) limit.

----- SYSTEM PROMPT (paste into the system/custom-instructions field) -----
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
You are an expert in wastewater treatments.

Inputs:
A wastewater treatment plant permit extract
Ontology lists: equipments, processes and roles (with mandatory relationships)

Task:
Using only the wastewater treatment plant's permit extract, find all the future, implemented or past treatments mentioned to be used in the Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility facility. A treatment must be described by at least an equipment or a process from the given ontology lists.
You may ignore irrelevant context.
Think step by step:
1. Extract explicitly from the permit text the treatments mentioned for the specific Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility facility (if it is used in another facility or off-site it should be mentionned in the "location" field of the output JSON)
2. Match each treatment find with an equipment and/or one or many processes from the ontology lists
3. Complete with specification of the treatment with roles and substances if explicitly mentioned in the text
4. Determine if the treatment is currently implemented and used in the facility ("present"), planned for the future ("future") or has been shut or removed ("past")
5. Structure the output as a JSON file as requested

Output:
Your entire response must be a single JSON object: an "items" array with one object per treatment unit, using this structure:
{
    "items": [
        {
            "Equipment": string|null,
            "Process": string[]|null,
            "Role": string[]|null,
            "Substance": string[]|null,
            "Implementation": "present"|"future"|"past",
            "Location": "on-site"|"off-site"|null,
            "Sentence": string
        }
    ]
}
Emit one object per distinct treatment step. A municipal plant typically yields ~8-20 steps. Each object must include exactly one Equipment (or one Process if no equipment is named), plus only the processes that occur in that unit. Processes in different units belong in different objects.

Field meaning and allowed cardinality:
- Equipment: equipment name from ontology. Exactly one string, or null only if no specific equipment is identifiable. Populate whenever a physical unit or vessel is mentioned or clearly implied.
- Process: process names from ontology. Null or an array of 1+ strings. Populate whenever a treatment process is described, even if Equipment is also set.
- Role: role names from ontology. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Substance: substance names explicitly mentioned in text. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Rule: at least one of Equipment or Process must be non-null. Both should be set when both are relevant.
- Implementation: a single string, one of "present", "future", "past".
- Location: a single string, one of "on-site" or "off-site"
- Sentence: shortest excerpt supporting the extraction from the permit — a phrase, not necessarily a full sentence.

All equipment, process, role and substance names must be exactly as listed in the ontology sections (EQUIPMENTS, PROCESSES, ROLES, SUBSTANCES). Use only the most specific applicable term; its parents are inferred, so do not also list them (e.g. "CentrifugalThickener", not "CentrifugalThickener" and "Thickener").

Do not include example processes listed in boilerplate language.

Sludge-MixedLiquor | Mixed liquor is the mixture of wastewater and activated sludge in the aeration basin of a wastewater treatment plant. It contains suspended solids and water. | SubSubstanceOf: Fluid-Sludge

Example 1:
Permit text extract: "2.1.3. Wastewater Treatment. The treatment plant provides treatment consisting of influent screening and grit removal, primary clarification, advanced secondary treatment with an activated sludge process configured and operated for biological nutrient removal (BNR), secondary clarifiers, mono-media gravity filtration, and disinfection with chlorine (sodium hypochlorite), and dechlorination (sodium bisulfite).
    2.1.3.5. Disinfection. Sodium hypochlorite and ammonia are metered into the filter effluent at the head of four serpentine chlorine contact channels to produce a solution of chloramines for disinfection. As the effluent leaves the contact channels, its chlorine residual is measured, and an appropriate amount of sodium bisulfite is added to neutralize the hypochlorite.
    2.1.4. Sludge and Biosolids Management. Sludge from primary and secondary clarification operations is processed using Temperature Phased Anaerobic Digestion (TPAD) with thermophilic and mesophilic anaerobic digesters that are operated in series. The process consists of primary sludge screening, dissolved air flotation (DAF) thickening of primary and secondary sludges, and TPAD. Digested sludge then undergoes dewatering using open air drying beds or mechanical dewatering using centrifuges.
    Planned Changes: 2.5.2. Digested Sludge Dewatering Facility. This project will build a new digested sludge dewatering facility, including centrifuges and polymer storage and dosing equipment."

Find all the treatment processes explicitely used in the Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility facility. Here is the permit extract:
        [Page 3]
24 pump stations, 18 miles of force mains, and serves 4 million residents and
several cities and agencies under contractual agreements. Approximately 85% of
HWRP’s influent flow consists of residential, commercial, and industrial
ADOPTED: 10/19/2023 4
[Page 4]
City of Los Angeles Bureau of Sanitation Order No. R4-2023-0357
Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility File No. 23-104
wastewater from the City of Los Angeles; the remaining 15% comes from the
contract cities and agencies.
The HWRP is a full secondary wastewater treatment plant designed to treat a
maximum daily dry weather flow of 450 million gallons per day (mgd) and a peak
wet weather flow of 850 mgd. The treatment process at the HWRP consists of
preliminary treatment (bar screens, grit chamber), primary treatment
(coagulation, flocculation, primary sedimentation), secondary treatment
(activated sludge and clarification), and solids handling. The secondary-treated
effluent from HWRP is discharged to the Santa Monica Bay and is regulated
under National Pollutant Discharge Elimination System (NPDES) Permit No.
CA0109991.
1.3. Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility
The HAWPF will have the capacity to treat up to 2 mgd of primary-treated
wastewater from the HWRP to produce up to 1.5 mgd of advanced-treated
recycled water (recycled water). The process flow diagram is depicted in
Attachment B. The HAWPF treatment process will consist of fine screens,
membrane bioreactors (MBR), reverse osmosis (RO), and an ultraviolet (UV)
disinfection/advanced oxidation process (UV/AOP), and post-treatment
stabilization. The HAWPF will also include multiple pump stations, a chemical
storage facility, a 125,000-gallon product water tank, and a distribution network.
During normal operation, 100% of the recycled water will be beneficially reused
for non-potable applications at LAX and within HWRP.
Preliminary Treatment – After primary treatment at the HWRP, the primary
effluent is conveyed to the Intermediate Pump Station East and West (IPS-E &
IPS-W), a portion of which will be conveyed to the HAWPF for advanced
treatment. A set of two pumps will be installed on the operating floor of each side
of the IPS influent channel. Each pair of pumps will be capable of drawing water
from the respective primary effluent channel. Lines from IPS-E and IPS-W
channels will each be equipped with a flowmeter and conductivity and ammonia
probes. Fine screens will be provided upstream of the MBR tanks to remove
particulate material to avoid damaging the membranes. A horizontal-packaged
drum screen system with a sluiceway for screening disposal will be used to
control odors. The screenings will be periodically dislodged from the screens and
conveyed to the waste return pump station and back to the HWRP IPS afterbay.
MBR Process - The MBR system will consist of two parallel biological nutrient
removal tanks and three parallel membrane tanks. The MBR process will consist
of biological treatment utilizing anoxic, aerobic, and deoxygenation tanks,
followed by submerged membrane ultrafiltration. The MBR process will remove
carbonaceous biochemical oxygen demand, nitrogen through nitrification and
denitrification, and microbial solids removal through membrane separation. The
biological treatment tanks will consist of two baffled anoxic zones followed by two
aerobic zones. A deoxygenation zone will be included upstream of the anoxic
zones so that residual dissolved oxygen from the membrane tanks will be
consumed biologically before returning to the anoxic zones. Membrane
ADOPTED: 10/19/2023 5
[Page 5]
City of Los Angeles Bureau of Sanitation Order No. R4-2023-0357
Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility File No. 23-104
separation to remove microbial solids will consist of three parallel covered
membrane tanks, membranes, and MBR auxiliary equipment.
RO Process - RO removes large molecular weight dissolved organic and
inorganic compounds and pathogens by forcing MBR effluent through a
semipermeable membrane under a higher pressure than the osmotic pressure of
the solution. The HAWPF RO system will consist of an RO feed tank, feed
pumps, pretreatment (chloramine injection, sulfuric acid and antiscalant addition),
cartridge filters, two RO trains, and RO membrane cleaning and flushing. There
will be two RO trains, each with a design capacity of 0.75 mgd, which will operate
continuously at a constant flux. Each RO train will have two stages designed to
achieve a recovery of up to 85% and to produce 0.75 mgd of RO permeate. RO
feed pumps will pump MBR effluent stored in the RO feed tank through cartridge
filters and into the RO membranes. The RO concentrated stream will be
conveyed back to the HWRP IPS afterbay. Permeate from the RO membranes
will flow to the UV/AOP treatment process. Chloramine will be injected after the
RO feed tank to prevent biological fouling of the RO membranes. Sulfuric acid
and antiscalant will be injected after the RO feed tank to control mineral scaling
of the RO membranes. Cartridge filters will be provided upstream of the RO
membranes to remove particulate matter. Chemical cleaning and removal of
particles, mineral scale, and biological film from the RO membrane will be
performed to avoid head loss and the resulting increase in energy use.
UV/AOP - The primary functions of UV/AOP include pathogen inactivation and
destruction and removal of trace organic compounds. The UV/AOP process will
provide additional treatment to control taste and odor compounds if needed.
Chloramine will be dosed upstream of the UV reactors (two on duty and one
standby) to generate hydroxyl radicals to eliminate the need for an additional
chemical onsite and to provide a chlorine residual to achieve additional
disinfection in the effluent pipeline. The UV system will use low-pressure high-
output lamps with an emissions wavelength peak at about 254 nanometers (nm).
For non-potable water reuse applications using RO permeate, the California
State Water Resources Control Board (State Water Board) Division of Drinking
Water (DDW) requires a UV dose of 50 millijoules per square centimeter
(mJ/cm2) under conditions with fouled lamp sleeves and aged UV lamps. This will
provide 5-log removal of poliovirus, which is the California virus reduction
requirement for non-potable water reuse. Considering potential future application
of the HAWPF product for potable reuse, the UV dose planned for the HAWPF
will be scalable so that it can meet at least the non-potable reuse set point of 50
mJ/cm2 but also achieve potable reuse requirements (not less than 800 mJ/cm2).
Post-treatment Stabilization - Post-treatment stabilization includes calcite
contactors, which will be located upstream of the product water pump station and
will be used to adjust pH, add alkalinity and hardness, and stabilize the water
quality to prevent adverse effects within the distribution system. Calcite and
sodium hydroxide will be used for adjustment of pH, alkalinity, and hardness.
Sodium hypochlorite and liquid ammonium sulfate will be added post-stabilization
ADOPTED: 10/19/2023 6
[Page 6]
City of Los Angeles Bureau of Sanitation Order No. R4-2023-0357
Hyperion Advanced Water Purification Facility File No. 23-104
to provide a 2 milligrams per liter (mg/L) chloramine residual in the product water
tank and the distribution piping.
Odor Control - Odor from three foul air streams (fine screens, biological nutrient
removal basins, and MBR tanks) will be captured, combined, and conveyed to a
single odor control unit consisting of a single-bed carbon adsorber, which will
remove hydrogen sulfide and a variety of organic sulfides that are commonly
emitted from primary effluent screens and the biological nutrient removal and
MBR processes.

be a 125,000-gallon cast-in-place concrete tank that will store the equivalent of
one production train at 0.75 mgd for four hours. The product water export pump
station will pump the recycled water into the water distribution network for export
to LAX. Vertical turbine pumps installed on the deck of the product water tank will
pump product water to LAX, the HWRP indus

----- SYSTEM PROMPT (paste into the system/custom-instructions field) -----
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
You are an expert in wastewater treatments.

Inputs:
A wastewater treatment plant permit extract
Ontology lists: equipments, processes and roles (with mandatory relationships)

Task:
Using only the wastewater treatment plant's permit extract, find all the future, implemented or past treatments mentioned to be used in the South Entrance Wastewater Facility facility. A treatment must be described by at least an equipment or a process from the given ontology lists.
You may ignore irrelevant context.
Think step by step:
1. Extract explicitly from the permit text the treatments mentioned for the specific South Entrance Wastewater Facility facility (if it is used in another facility or off-site it should be mentionned in the "location" field of the output JSON)
2. Match each treatment find with an equipment and/or one or many processes from the ontology lists
3. Complete with specification of the treatment with roles and substances if explicitly mentioned in the text
4. Determine if the treatment is currently implemented and used in the facility ("present"), planned for the future ("future") or has been shut or removed ("past")
5. Structure the output as a JSON file as requested

Output:
Your entire response must be a single JSON object: an "items" array with one object per treatment unit, using this structure:
{
    "items": [
        {
            "Equipment": string|null,
            "Process": string[]|null,
            "Role": string[]|null,
            "Substance": string[]|null,
            "Implementation": "present"|"future"|"past",
            "Location": "on-site"|"off-site"|null,
            "Sentence": string
        }
    ]
}
Emit one object per distinct treatment step. A municipal plant typically yields ~8-20 steps. Each object must include exactly one Equipment (or one Process if no equipment is named), plus only the processes that occur in that unit. Processes in different units belong in different objects.

Field meaning and allowed cardinality:
- Equipment: equipment name from ontology. Exactly one string, or null only if no specific equipment is identifiable. Populate whenever a physical unit or vessel is mentioned or clearly implied.
- Process: process names from ontology. Null or an array of 1+ strings. Populate whenever a treatment process is described, even if Equipment is also set.
- Role: role names from ontology. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Substance: substance names explicitly mentioned in text. Null or an array (can be empty).
- Rule: at least one of Equipment or Process must be non-null. Both should be set when both are relevant.
- Implementation: a single string, one of "present", "future", "past".
- Location: a single string, one of "on-site" or "off-site"
- Sentence: shortest excerpt supporting the extraction from the permit — a phrase, not necessarily a full sentence.

All equipment, process, role and substance names must be exactly as listed in the ontology sections (EQUIPMENTS, PROCESSES, ROLES, SUBSTANCES). Use only the most specific applicable term; its parents are inferred, so do not also list them (e.g. "CentrifugalThickener", not "CentrifugalThickener" and "Thickener").

Do not include example processes listed in boilerplate language.

PROCESSES:
Process | A process applied to water treatment.
PhysicalProcess | A unit process that relies on physical forces to separate or treat water.
Separation | The most generic process for separation of any two constituents. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Elutriation | A process that uses a stream of fluid flowing upward to separate a mixture of particles based on their size, shape, and density. | SubProcessOf: Separation

Sedimentation | A unit process that allows solids to settle out of water by gravity. | SubProcessOf: Separation

RapidSandFiltration | A filtration process that uses a bed of sand to remove impurities at high flow rates. | SubProcessOf: MediaFiltration
Flotation | A unit process that uses buoyancy to separate solids from water, often using air bubbles. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
GasTransfer | A unit process that transfers gases, such as oxygen or carbon dioxide, into or out of water. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess
Aeration | A unit process that aerates (i.e., transfers oxygen into water). | SubProcessOf: GasTransfer
Mixing | A unit process that mixes water with chemicals or other substances to achieve a desired effect. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess

Thickening | A process to increase the solids content of a sludge or slurry. | SubProcessOf: PhysicalProcess

Examples of treatments mentioned in permit text and the corresponding JSON output:

Example 1:
Permit text extract: "2.1.3. Wastewater Treatment. The treatment plant provides treatment consisting of influent screening and grit removal, primary clarification, advanced secondary treatment with an activated sludge process configured and operated for biological nutrient removal (BNR), secondary clarifiers, mono-media gravity filtration, and disinfection with chlorine (sodium hypochlorite), and dechlorination (sodium bisulfite).
    2.1.3.5. Disinfection. Sodium hypochlorite and ammonia are metered into the filter effluent at the head of four serpentine chlorine contact channels to produce a solution of chloramines for disinfection. As the effluent leaves the contact channels, its chlorine residual is measured, and an appropriate amount of sodium bisulfite is added to neutralize the hypochlorite.
    2.1.4. Sludge and Biosolids Management. Sludge from primary and secondary clarification operations is processed using Temperature Phased Anaerobic Digestion (TPAD) with thermophilic and mesophilic anaerobic digesters that are operated in series. The process consists of primary sludge screening, dissolved air flotation (DAF) thickening of primary and secondary sludges, and TPAD. Digested sludge then undergoes dewatering using open air drying beds or mechanical dewatering using centrifuges.
    Planned Changes: 2.5.2. Digested Sludge Dewatering Facility. This project will build a new digested sludge dewatering facility, including centrifuges and polymer storage and dosing equipment."

Find all the treatment processes explicitely used in the South Entrance Wastewater Facility facility. Here is the permit extract:
        [Page 1]
Base and Meridian in Mariposa County.
The existing OWTS at the South Entrance Station consists of flush toilets at the “Ranger
office” and the “Historic Comfort Station”. The septic tanks at the existing areas will be
replaced and a new “Visitor Contact Center” will be constructed. The new septic tanks
at the Historic Comfort Center and the Ranger office will have capacities of 15,000
gallons and 2,000 gallons, respectively. The existing fixtures at the Historic Comfort
Station and the Ranger office will be replaced by low flow plumbing fixtures. The septic
tank for the new Visitor Contact Center will have a 55,000 gallon capacity. New 6-inch
PVC pipe will convey effluent from all three new septic tanks to a 20,000 gallon dosing
tank for flow equalization. The effluent will be discharged from the dosing tank into a
new 3-acre leachfield southwest of the South Entrance Station along an existing service
road that is to be improved to provide all year access. The new Visitor Contact Center
will be equipped with 17 low-flow toilets, three waterless urinals, and 10 low-flow sinks.
The proposed leachfield will cover about 3.0 acres and will consist of a pressure
distribution leach trench constructed in four cells. Each cell is equipped with its own
6-outlet automatic, SCADA monitored distributing valve, and each cell consists of six
zones typically with two 120-foot laterals. Each leachfield cell will have an inspection
port in each leachfield lateral for monitoring wastewater depth in the leach trenches.
Two groundwater monitoring wells will be installed at the base of the leachfield to
measure the depth to water below the leachfield area.
The anticipated average daily flow from the new Visitor Contact Center will be about
9,500 gallons per day (gpd) with peak flows of 15,830 gpd on holidays. The average
flow anticipated from the Historic Comfort Station will be about 2,300 gpd with a peak
flow of 3,890 gpd. The peak flow at the Ranger office will be 200 gpd.
